ANNUAL REPORT 2026
  We are a borderless,
  integrated network
that spans time zones
    and continents.
   And never sleeps.
      The connectedness of our network and
     motivation of our people powers us to find
          efficiencies and opportunities.

Connection and consistency                               Harness our agility
Offer customers a connected, continuous network          We have the scale of international infrastructure
providing vital links between small towns, major         and resources, yet the agility and autonomy to
cities and capitals of the world. We strive to provide   adapt as conditions change. By understanding
consistent quality, technologies and service across      our customers’ complex needs and priorities at
every branch and region                                  a local and international level, we can customise
                                                         integrated solutions quickly and decisively
Operate as one seamless business
We draw on our international ‘brains trust’ to           Honour our 100 year journey
evaluate local conditions and risks and utilise          Back-to-back adverse conditions in recent years
every corner of our network. We want to give             have seen the business learn, grow and adapt.
customers every reason to work with us                   Expecting the unexpected is embedded in our
                                                         culture. In 50 or 100 years from now, we will look
Stay aspirational and focused                            back on these as some of our most important
Learn from disruptions; be meticulous about              and formative years.
quality; find efficiencies; continue to seek out
customers who trade internationally and fit
our business



  The agility, decisiveness and vision that’s
powered us for 48 years, continues to underpin
   the ‘what’ and ‘how’ of everything we do.
                                                                                                              KAYLA COSTA AND THOMAS MALONI, EPPING, MELBOURNE, AUSTRALIA

NOTICE OF MEETING Notice is given that the annual meeting of shareholders of Mainfreight Limited will be held
at 4.00pm on Thursday, 30 July 2026 in the Level 4 Lounge, South Stand, Eden Park, Reimers Avenue, Kingsland,
Auckland, New Zealand. Full details, including the Meeting Agenda, are contained in the separate Notice of
Meeting and Explanatory Notes accompanying this report, and are also available on the Company’s website,
www.mainfreight.com, or by scanning the QR code to the left.

         As our business and energy requirements
         expand, so too is the scale and sophistication
         of our energy capture and storage infrastructure.
         Our new Willawong facility in Queensland, Australia has been designed from the ground up,
         with a focus on our future energy requirements. Supported by on-site solar and battery storage,
         Willawong includes 480kW of on-dock fast charging, 480kW multi-vehicle charging station
         and eight 22kW AC chargers for smaller fleet vehicles.

         As we incorporate smart charging infrastructure, renewables, and battery storage into our sites,
         Mainfreight branches around the world are becoming both freight hubs and energy stations.                          GREG DE LAUTOUR WITH OUR BATTERY ENERGY

     1   CORPORATE INFORMATION                                                                                                                In May 2024, the XRB issued NZ IFRS 18, which replaces NZ IAS 1 Presentation of Financial Statements. IFRS 18 introduces new
     The financial statements of Mainfreight Limited (“the Parent”) and its subsidiaries (“the Group”) for the year ended 31 March 2026       requirements for presentation within the statement of profit or loss, including specified totals and subtotals. Furthermore, entities
     were authorised for issue in accordance with a resolution of the Directors.                                                              are required to classify all income and expenses within the statement of profit or loss into one of five categories: operating,
                                                                                                                                              investing, financing, income taxes and discontinued operations, whereof the first three are new.
     Mainfreight Limited is a company limited by shares incorporated in New Zealand whose shares are publicly traded on the NZX
     Main Board (New Zealand Stock Exchange).                                                                                                 It also requires disclosure of newly defined management-defined performance measures, subtotals of income and expenses, and

                                                                                                                                              primary financial statements (PFS) and the notes.

     Level 1: the fair value is calculated using quoted prices in active markets,                                                              Total Liabilities            586,021        647,847          546,257       53,206        497,833         (70,880)    2,260,284
     Level 2: the fair value is estimated using inputs other than quoted prices included in Level 1 that are observable for the asset or
     liability, either directly (as prices) or indirectly (derived from prices),
     Level 3: the fair value is estimated using inputs for the asset or liability that are not based on observable market data.
     The methods for estimating fair value are outlined in the relevant notes to the financial statements. Refer to note 12.

     The Profit before Taxation is stated:                                                                                                                       Current tax assets and liabilities for the current and prior periods are measured at the amount expected to be recovered from or
                                                                                                                                                                 paid to the taxation authorities based on the current period’s taxable income. The tax rates and tax laws used to compute the
                                                                                                                                   2026              2025        amount are those that are enacted or substantively enacted by the balance sheet date.
                                                                                                                                 NZ$000            NZ$000
                                                                                                                                                                 Deferred income tax is generally provided on all temporary differences at the balance sheet date between the tax bases of assets
     After Charging :                                                                                                                                            and liabilities and their carrying amounts for financial reporting purposes.
     Audit Fees – Parent Company Auditors *                                                                                        3,113            3,176        The carrying amount of deferred income tax assets is reviewed at each balance sheet date and reduced to the extent that it is no
                                                                                                                                                                 longer probable that sufficient taxable profit will be available to allow all or part of the deferred income tax asset to be utilised.
     Audit Fees – Other Auditors                                                                                                     201               236
                                                                                                                                                                 Unrecognised deferred income tax assets are reassessed at each balance sheet date and are recognised to the extent that it has
     Total Audit Related Services                                                                                                  3,314            3,412        become probable that future taxable profit will allow the deferred tax asset to be recovered.
                                                                                                                                                                 Deferred tax assets and deferred tax liabilities are offset only if a legally enforceable right exists to set off current tax assets against
                                                                                                                                                                 current tax liabilities and the deferred tax assets and liabilities relate to the same taxable entity and the same taxation authority.
     Report on Historical Financial Information (Agreed upon Procedures)                                                               15                 –
                                                                                                                                                                 The OECD/G20 Inclusive Framework on BEPS addresses tax challenges from digitalisation of the global economy. The Pillar Two
     Sustainability Reporting Assurance **                                                                                             98               75       model rules apply a 15% global minimum tax to multinational enterprises (MNEs) with annual revenue over EUR750 million. These
                                                                                                                                                                 rules have been enacted in several countries in which the Group operates, including the Netherlands, Belgium, Australia, and
     Total other assurance services and other agreed-upon procedures engagement                                                      113                75
                                                                                                                                                                 others, effective from 1 January 2024 and New Zealand, where the Group is head quartered, from 1 January 2025. The Group
                                                                                                                                                                 qualifies as a MNE under these rules.
                                                                                                                                                                 The Group has adopted amendments to NZ IAS 12 Income Tax, effective 10 August 2023, which includes:
     Other Fees Paid to Parent Company Auditors *
                                                                                                                                                                 –   A temporary exception to the recognition and disclosure of deferred taxes from Pillar Two rules.
     Tax Advice                                                                                                                      294               124
                                                                                                                                                                 –   Disclosure requirements to help users understand the Group’s exposure to Pillar Two income taxes.
     Transfer Pricing                                                                                                                591               947
                                                                                                                                                                 The Group has assessed its potential exposure to Pillar Two taxes based on 2025 and 2026 financial information including the
     Tax Compliance                                                                                                                1,008               817       transitory safe harbours rules.

     Total Tax Services                                                                                                            1,893            1,888        As a result, the effective tax rates in significant jurisdictions where the Group operates are above 15%, so no significant current tax

     The element of this valuation related to land has been recorded in the financial statements resulting in the revaluation of land by        Both.
     $335,797,000 (2025 $321,768,000) above cost. In addition a deferred tax liability of $54,196,000 (2025 $50,087,000) was recorded
     resulting in a revaluation reserve of $281,601,000 (2025 $271,681,000)
     The element of this valuation related to buildings has not been recorded in the financial statements. If it had been recorded
     a revaluation in buildings of $262,982,000 (2025 $252,530,000) would have occurred. In addition, a deferred tax liability of
     $22,500,000 (2025 $21,207,000) would have needed to be recorded resulting in an increase in the revaluation reserve of
     $240,483,000 (2025 $231,323,000)
     In determining the fair value of land, the valuers have considered relevant general and economic factors and in particular have
     investigated recent sales and leasing transactions of comparable properties that have occurred in the relevant locations within
     which the assets sit. The valuers have used two principal approaches which are a capitalisation analysis and a direct comparison
     approach. The valuations of land have been determined using the direct comparison approach to value each parcel of land and as
     a result these are considered level 2 valuations.
     Included in the Group book values above but not in the valuations are Leasehold Improvements of $119,884,000 (2025 $91,057,000).

                                                                                                                                                           (iii) Key assumptions used in value in use calculations for cash generating units (CGU’s)
                                                                                                                                                           The calculation of value in use for all CGU’s is most sensitive to the following assumptions; EBITDA (adjusted), discount rates and
                                                                                                                                                           growth rates used.
                                                                                                                                                           EBITDA (adjusted) is based on forecasts.
                                                                                                                                                           Discount rates reflect management’s estimate of the time value of money and the risks specific to each unit.
                                                                                                                                                           For the purposes of impairment testing a terminal growth rate has been used for all segments based on the long-term industry
                                                                                                                                                           and country averages.
                                                                                                                                                           (iv) Sensitivity to changes in assumptions
                                                                                                                                                           With regard to the assessment of the value in use for all CGU’s, management believes that no reasonably possible change in any
                                                                                                                                                           of the above key assumptions would cause the carrying values of the units to materially exceed its recoverable amount.

     GROUP MANAGING DIRECTORS REMUNERATION FOR THE YEAR ENDING 31 MARCH 2026.
                                                                                                                                                      TOTAL DIRECTORS                                                                 19,877,658   19,926,091
     This section details the remuneration earned by the Group Managing Director (GMD), Don Braid for the year ending
     31 March 2026
                                                                                                                                                      Directors’ shareholdings at balance date were 19.74% of total shares issued.
     Don Braid’s overall remuneration consists of Fixed Annual Remuneration (FAR), a Discretionary Bonus and a Short Term
     Incentive (STI).
     FAR is reviewed every two years in October by the Remuneration Committee, and approved by the Board.
     The Discretionary Bonus consists of team profit share bonus and a one week Christmas Bonus, based on company performance.
     The STI is reviewed annually by the Remuneration Committee, and approved by the Board.
     The at risk portion of the STI is 33% of base FAR
     The performance targets for the GMD STI scheme for the year ending 31 March 2026 were as follows

      Target KPIs                                                                                Weighting

      Profit before Tax Growth                                                                      20%
      Revenue Growth                                                                                20%
      Return on Revenue                                                                             15%
      Quality                                                                                       15%
      People Development                                                                            15%
      Culture and Supply Chain Development                                                          15%

     last two years.
